- **MUST-PAUSE (Task 0.1, practitioner ratification), 2026-11-08T09:40+11:00 — CLOSED 2026-11-08T09:50+11:00: decided (a), the practitioner's words "0.1: (a) the drafted list as is, go with recommended" (on Task 0.1).** The brief as put: options (a) the drafted list as is; (b) the drafted list with the practitioner's additions / removals, each named; rec=executor:(a), once the composer's audit run exits 0 — every entry is a shipped abbreviation with its one standard expansion, a lay and a clinical name for the same body part, or a week count, and every abbreviation that could read as an everyday word or has two meanings is left out. **In plain English:** this list is the only shorthand the two prose writing styles (not Clean clinical) will be allowed to use. When a line says "lower back pain", the prose may write "LBP"; "three weeks" may become "3/52"; "kneecap" may become "patella" — and the safety check treats the two forms as the same words, so nothing else gets through. It holds 93 entries: 49 body parts (41 of your shipped abbreviations such as ACL, SIJ, TMJ, plus 8 everyday names such as kneecap → patella and shoulder blade → scapula), 4 complaint words (LBP, PGP, C/O "complains of", and pins and needles → paraesthesia), 27 examination terms (ROM, SLR, VAS, NWB and the like), "both sides" / bilateral → B/L, and the twelve week counts 1/52 to 12/52 (days and months stay as spoken, as decided). Of your 168 shipped abbreviations, 73 are used; the other 95 are left out: 30 because they are also everyday words and a check could mistake one for the other ("AS" is "as", "US" is "us", and also SOB, MET, Post, Traps, Add, Ant, ART, Flex, Glut, Hams, Mob, NAGS, PA, PROM, Quads, Rot, SNAGS, Sup, TA, TENS, WAD — plus seven I found: Ev "EV", HEP "hep B", HR "hr" for hour, Lat "lat" the gym muscle, Med "med", OT overtime, TP); 11 because they have two common meanings (Abd, Bx, Ext, IR, LSJ, LT, UT, PF plantarflexion or pelvic floor, Px, STM massage or mobilisation, Sx symptoms or surgery); 4 because their expansion contains "no", "on" or "within", which the check guards separately (NAD, NKDA, O/E, WNL); L and R (sides); ER (the check ignores "er" as a filler); Dist and Prox (they would clash with DIPJ and PIPJ); DOMS ("soreness" is on the never-swap list); and 44 treatment, diagnosis, history, test and referral abbreviations (HVLA, OA, Hx, MRI, GP and the like), because the design's five kinds of entry (body part, complaint, examination term, side, weeks) have no place for them — adding a sixth kind would change the locked design (D1) and would be a separate dated decision, not part of this list. Feelings words (sore, ache, tight, stiff, dull, sharp, numb, swelling, and 15 more such as tingling, burning, swollen) are on a never-swap list, so "sore" can never become "pain" and "swelling" never "effusion". To choose (b), name each entry to add or remove; an addition must be a by-definition equivalent (an abbreviation and its expansion, or a lay and a clinical name for the same thing).

- **MUST-PAUSE (Task C.0 `[decision]` — the suggestion window's size and whether placed neighbouring lines go in as context), 2026-11-09T04:44:25+11:00 — CLOSED: DECIDED (a) by the practitioner, "continue, go with recommended" (2026-11-09T04:38+11:00, given before these options were shown — the circumstance on Task C.0); recorded on Task C.0 and as D9's dated note by leg x2.** The derivation is the dated sub-bullet on Task C.0 (every input an estimate, named: the suggestion instruction is not written yet, the segment rate is the Phase 2 benchmark's, the share of left-out lines is assumed). Options: (a) `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 40`, context lines on — a window ≈ 36 s; a 25-minute consultation needs ≈ 315 s, so the 240 s cap stops it after 7 windows (≈ 250 s) with its last ≈ 5 minutes not assessed; ≤ 20 minutes assessed whole; (b) `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 100`, context lines on — a window ≈ 74 s; 25 minutes assessed whole in ≈ 265 s (≤ 28 minutes); (c) context lines off, `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 25` asked lines — a window ≈ 36 s; 25 minutes assessed whole in ≈ 160 s, short answers routed without their question. rec=executor:(a) — the 30–45 s target is met, the cap cuts only the end of a long consultation (mostly your own already-warned lines), and the context is what routes a short answer; (c) is the fair alternative if every line being looked at matters more. **In plain English:** after a consultation the app can ask the local model where each line it left out of the note belongs, and mark its guess beside that line in the "Add a line" list — you still choose and press Add. The model reads the transcript in chunks; this decides the chunk size, and whether each chunk also shows the lines around the left-out ones (with the section they already went to) so a short answer like "yeah, about three weeks" can be matched to its question. All the guesses appear at once when the model finishes. With (a) that is about 4 minutes after review starts for a 25-minute consultation (about 2 minutes for a 10-minute one); the last few minutes of a long consultation are not looked at — they stay as today, left out with their warning, and the count line says how many. With (b) everything is looked at in about 4½ minutes, but each chunk takes about 74 seconds. With (c) everything is looked at in about 2¾ minutes, but each left-out line is judged on its own, without the lines around it, so short answers are guessed less well. Complete and Discard never wait under any option (they stop the model's work at once). What does wait is at most one chunk: closing the app, opening another recording from the Recovery tab, or Save after you add a line in a prose writing style — about 36 seconds under (a) or (c), about 74 under (b); for that same time the transcript text stays in the computer's memory after Complete or Discard. These are estimates from the laptop's measured speed, which varied by about a third between evenings; the real numbers are measured later (C.6, and the live smoke).

Run 9 uses `confirm-all` (D11, which already rejects changing the rule for suggestions), so run 9 is now more likely to fail on that limit; predictions v6 will say so. ## Review Findings Log
### Round 1 — 2026-11-08
Source: Codex — codex gpt-6-astra high — plan peer-review

**PR-HIGH-001 — The register’s ordinary-word collision rule remains incomplete.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:164` principally excludes function words, but `clinical_abbreviations.json:144` includes `SOB`, whose folded form also means a crying sound; other omissions include `MET` (`:87`), `Post` (`:119`) and `Traps` (`:156`). A symptom entry for SOB and its clinical expansion satisfies the specified invariants, yet symmetric folding would permit the ordinary word to acquire the clinical meaning. This is a newly admitted meaning change, not merely the existing attachment residue.  
Proposed amendment: Require an ordinary-word collision audit of every proposed term before ratification, extending the pinned exclusions and regression cases beyond function words.

**PR-MED-001 — Rendering reuse does not bind the new frame eligibility.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:135` retains text-only binding. Editing the sole patient transcript line without changing its wording creates a clinician-provenance assertion without a speaker (`ui/note.py:1584`, `:1660`), making `other_voice` false while retaining the same digest (`note.py:1084`). `ui/models.py:4137` consequently carries the previously passed patient-framed rendering; arriving results have the same problem at `:4129`.  
Proposed amendment: Revalidate carried and arriving renderings against current frame eligibility, with same-text replacement and Undo tests, without adding a persisted field.

**PR-MED-002 — The proposed busy flag does not guard the cited Chrome or destructive paths.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: D8/C.4 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:171`, `:243`) extend `NoteScreen.is_busy`, but `ChromeBridge._screen` is a `SessionScreen` (`ui/bridge.py:261`, `:274`), and the cited guards read that screen (`:953`, `:1017`, `:1091`). There is also no Chrome Complete command (`protocol.py:148`). Desktop completion already destroys the key before `note_screen.clear()` (`ui/main_window.py:2070`, `:2075`), contradicting C13 while agreeing with the plan’s post-key plaintext residue at line 141.  
Proposed amendment: Define and wire the exit policy at the actual desktop and Chrome entry points, reconcile C13 with the accepted plaintext lifetime, and replace the nonexistent panel-Complete smoke with tests through real window/bridge wiring.

**PR-MED-003 — A whole-job busy flag cannot provide the promised one-window wait.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: Flow 2 and C.0 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:158`, `:239`) describe progress after the current window returns, but C.1 runs multiple windows inside one job (`:240`), and `ui/note.py:2828` remains busy until the job and its orphans finish. Even after correcting the guard wiring, the wait covers the whole stage, including yield waits and the final window beyond the cap check.  
Proposed amendment: Specify either cancellation of remaining windows for a pending exit or an explicitly accepted whole-stage wait, and verify the chosen behavior with a multi-window job.

**PR-MED-004 — The specified clock cannot exclude model-lock waits.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: D9/C.1 (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:172`, `:240`) exclude lock waits from the cap and “Model seconds,” but the interface returns only text or token counts (`language_model.py:115`), acquiring its private lock inside both calls (`:327`, `:353`). An external clock cannot distinguish waiting from model work, and no task adds that capability.  
Proposed amendment: Add a tested admission/timing seam that separates lock acquisition from measured work before implementing the cap and reporting its duration.

**PR-MED-005 — R.1 needs an executable import-time validation arrangement.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:230` places every semantic invariant in the `note_config` loader, but `note_check.py:273` already imports `note_config`; the required laterality, medication and polarity definitions appear afterward at `:316`, `:347` and `:1762`. Importing them back during configuration loading creates a cycle.  
Proposed amendment: Specify structural validation in `note_config` and semantic validation after the canonical classifiers exist, with tests covering both import entry points.

**PR-MED-006 — The headline first-person transformation cannot pass the specified gate.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: The Goal’s example (`.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:26`) drops first-person tokens that remain mandatory under `note_check.py:1843`; those tokens are absent from `prose_connectives.json`, and its interrupted anatomy phrase cannot match the proposed contiguous phrase fold. R.5 (`plan:234`) requires patient-spoken fixtures without requiring this representative construction.  
Proposed amendment: Add realistic first-person/contraction fixtures and state the achievable output under the fixed gate, requiring a separate decision before any widening needed to deliver the headline example.

**PR-MED-007 — Mandatory later tasks violate the prediction freeze.**

### Round 2 — 2026-11-08
Source: Codex — codex gpt-6-astra high — plan peer-review

**PR-HIGH-002 — Direct window close bypasses the amended exit policy.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:176` and `:330` exclude live suggestions from `is_busy` and put abort/orphaning in `clear()`. However, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:961` explicitly says accepted window close does not call `clear()`. After Save, the existing review guard is false (`:2828`), allowing the close guard at `ui/main_window.py:1478` to pass while the suggestion thread runs; `ui/note.py:2824` identifies destruction of a running QThread as a process-abort hazard.  
Proposed amendment: Add an explicit close-request cancellation/orphan transition before close eligibility is checked, retain the window until the worker finishes, and test closing directly after Save during both inference and a yield wait.

**PR-MED-009 — The digest amendment omits the shared producer/consumer contract.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:139`, `:171` and `:319` instruct changing `section_input_digest`, but its signature accepts only a section (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1084`), while frame eligibility also needs the note’s confirmed clinician. The shared stamping contract is `input_texts_digest` (`:1075`); changing only the section-side calculation would make freshly produced renderings fail `bound_rendering`’s comparison (`:1103`), including results handled through `ui/models.py:4129`.  
Proposed amendment: Specify one flag-aware digest used by rendering production and every binding/reuse path, thread the necessary note context through its callers, and test arriving results, carried results and the harness stage.

**PR-MED-010 — Duration data contradicts the canonical expansion.**  
Materiality: build-affecting

Proposed amendment: Put the numeric phrase first in each duration entry and pin exact expanded sequences, updating `:141` to distinguish the raw abbreviation’s date classification from the expanded numeral used by rule (d).

**PR-MED-011 — R.0’s recommended swap also removes modality.**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:316` and `:170` make `i'd` and `i'll` droppable while promising that the frame replaces “the first person and nothing else.” Those contractions also carry conditional/future meaning; current tokenisation preserves them (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:249`), and `note_check.py:1766` explicitly protects modal meaning against conversion into recorded fact. This consequence is not disclosed by the proposed decision.  
Proposed amendment: Preserve modal-bearing contractions or their modal component, describe that behavior accurately in R.0, and pin it without deciding the practitioner’s option.

Round 4 correctly identifies baseline failure against D5, but overstates what that establishes about future timings.

Findings:

- **PR-LOW-013** (LOW, ambiguity audit, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:477`): The retained-term audit omits common second meanings of `RC` (remote control), `ROM` (read-only memory), and `SLR` (single-lens reflex). Under D2’s unconditional symmetric fold, an input using one of these meanings can acquire the clinical expansion without triggering the token comparison. Round 4 LOW-003’s four named terms do not cover these entries. - Evidence: `"term": "RC", "phrases": ["rotator cuff"]`; line 517 retains `"ROM"` with `"range of motion"`; line 520 retains `"SLR"` with `"straight leg raise"`; line 431 names only `ANS`, `PNS`, `Cx`, and `C/O`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Extend LOW-003’s pending judgement to these three terms, recording acceptance or obtaining a ratified removal before R.1.

- **PR-LOW-014** (LOW, evidence overstatement, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:422`): The v2 measurements establish that the current baseline exceeds 5 seconds, not that v3 cannot meet that limit through prompt tuning. Added prompt tokens alone do not establish total completion time: clinical shorthand can shorten generated output, and Task 0.3 records a distinct, slower generation rate. The practitioner’s gate decision should not rest on a claimed impossibility that has not been measured. - Evidence: “R.5 cannot meet an absolute 5 s line on this CPU by re-tuning” and “would loop without end”; line 425 calls retaining D5 “unmeetable on this CPU.” Task 0.3 records prompt evaluation at 57.6 tokens/s and generation at 11.41 tokens/s. Recommendation: Fix-now - Preserve the baseline-failure finding and decision options, but describe v3 feasibility as unmeasured rather than impossible.

Looks good: the fold cannot move a protected or polarity fact — no entry may hold a polarity token, a polarity-bearing word, a laterality token (`_LATERALITY_TOKENS` is `{left, right}`), a medication, or a number or date outside `duration` (`note_check._register_refusal`), so a negation, side, dose or drug token is never consumed by a match, and rules (c) and (d) count tokens order-free (`_polarity_counts`, `_protected_counts`), which the fold's reordering cannot disturb; a changed number fails (the duration canonicals carry the numeral: "three weeks" ≡ `3` `weeks`, `2/52` → `2`); a different body part fails (every sibling pair differs in a canonical token — ACL / PCL, LCL / MCL, UMN / LMN, DIPJ / PIPJ, EHL / FHL, Cx / Lx, CTJ / TLJ); `non` stays in NWB's canonical, so NWB / FWB / PWB never meet. The widening is exactly D2's: a prose term ≡ its canonical phrase written out, which v2 already judged by tokens, plus the by-definition phrase equivalences and the spelled week counts (residue (6) on D.1). The loader refuses every malformed shape it is tested on (43 cases now), each match key has one owner, and longest match resolves the ASLR ⊃ SLR / AROM ⊃ ROM suffixes. `shorthand_line`'s location loop is correct (the matcher reports matches in order and keeps every unmatched token, so the first occurrence at or after the previous match's end IS the match), the withheld set is section-wide (the safe direction), and the phrase side is lexicon tokens only (never other words of a line). `section_other_voice` is the one derivation behind the prompt's Speaker line, the gate's frame and the stamped digest; the tagged digest cannot collide with the plain one, and an older (unflagged) rendering of a framed section is re-rendered, never shown (the safe direction for a recovered 0.3.5 session). Findings:

- **PR-LOW-015** (LOW, validation, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:1772`): Exact duplicate terms evade D1’s one-entry-per-term invariant. Two entries with `term="LBP"`, one containing `"lower back pain"` and another `"lumbar pain"`, pass ownership validation because their owner strings are equal. The lookup then retains the first term definition while accepting both phrase definitions. Even an identical repeated entry is accepted. Evidence: `owner = owners.setdefault(key, entry.term)` followed by `if owner != entry.term:`; lookup construction uses `lookup.setdefault((entry.term.lower(),), entry)`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Reject repeated folded terms before constructing ownership mappings, and test identical and conflicting duplicates with exactly matching case.
- **PR-LOW-016** (LOW, validation, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_config.py:1798`): Duplicate JSON object keys are silently discarded before validation. For example, an entry containing `"class":[],"class":"anatomy"` is accepted using the final value instead of producing the broken-install refusal; duplicated top-level fields behave similarly. Evidence: `payload = json.loads(resource.read_bytes())` uses no duplicate-key rejection hook. Recommendation: Fix-now - Reject duplicate object keys during decoding and test raw JSON at both entry and top-level boundaries.
- **PR-LOW-017** (LOW, testing, `desktop/tests/test_note_check.py:3143`): The new semantic-invariant tests would still pass if the production import-time `_refuse_register_overlap(CLINICAL_EQUIVALENTS)` call were removed. Invalid registers are checked by directly invoking the helper, while isolated-import tests only exercise the valid shipped register. Evidence: `note_check_module._refuse_register_overlap(CLINICAL_EQUIVALENTS)`, `_refuse(_equivalent("LKP", "left knee pain"))`, and the import test’s `assert result.returncode == 0`. Recommendation: Fix-now - Add an isolated import test with an injected structurally valid but semantically forbidden register, asserting the named broken-install failure.
- **PR-LOW-025** (LOW, record accuracy, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:747`): The record describes entry-wide withholding, but implementation withholds only the matched phrase/term pair. For `("six weeks ago", "Review in 6 weeks")`, the latter still offers `Shorthand: 6 weeks = 6/52`, although both phrases belong to the same duration entry. Evidence: the record says “WITHHOLDS a `duration` entry from the section when any occurrence of its phrase is followed directly by a point-in-time word”; `prose_style.py:439–448` instead uses `pair = f"{' '.join(match.matched)} = {match.entry.term}"`, `withheld.add(pair)` and `pair not in withheld`. Recommendation: Fix-now — describe the existing phrase-specific behavior accurately in the dated record and D.1 residue (3), and pin the mixed-alias case.
- **PR-LOW-026** (LOW, test coverage, `desktop/tests/test_prose_style.py:934`): The budget test does not distinguish the own-voice instruction from the narrative instruction. Both contain exactly the same words in different order, while the mock counts words. Reverting `conditioning_allowance` to always count `NARRATIVE_INSTRUCTION` would still satisfy this assertion. Evidence: `count = MockLanguageModel().count_tokens` followed by the expected `count(own)`; `prose_style.py:320–321` states “the mock counts words.” Recommendation: Fix-now — inject a counting spy that asserts which instruction it receives, or returns distinct counts for the two instructions; also pin the provider’s forwarding of `style=`.
- **PR-LOW-035** (LOW, record accuracy, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:684`): Attempt 2 incorrectly claims `narrative` meets D5 under every reading. Its shared-fixture mean alone is 7.32 s, above the fixed 6.7 s limit; including the nine new sections gives approximately 7.61 s. Evidence: “`narrative` MEETS D5 under every reading” versus table values “7.32 / 9.7” and “8.61 / 9.9”. Recommendation: Fix-now — qualify this historical verdict as meeting the paired reading only, and correct its handoff summary; attempt 5’s closure remains valid.
- **PR-LOW-036** (LOW, practitioner instructions, `docs/testing/prose-register-scoring.md:17`): The procedure omits creating its output directory and incorrectly places redirected summaries on the console. On a fresh run, a missing `C:\prose-register` prevents the command from starting; once created, both summaries enter the report before the JSON because the script prints them to stdout. Evidence: `--verbose > C:\prose-register\report.json`; line 20 says “The first lines on the console are one summary line per style”, and line 27 asks to compare against “the console’s count”. Recommendation: Fix-now — add the directory-creation step and describe the summary lines at the report’s beginning, including that the complete file is not standalone JSON.
- **PR-LOW-037** (LOW, documentation accuracy, `.cursor/plans/plan-note-content-design.md:764`): Residue (3) describes entry-wide withholding, but the implementation withholds an exact matched phrase–term pair. Another phrase belonging to the same duration entry can still be offered within that section. Evidence: “offers no `duration` entry for a section when any occurrence of its phrase is directly followed by a point-in-time word”; the code instead builds `pair = f"{' '.join(match.matched)} = {match.entry.term}"`, uses `withheld.add(pair)`, and filters by pair. Thus “six weeks ago” withholds `six weeks = 6/52`, while a separate “6 weeks” can still offer `6 weeks = 6/52`. Recommendation: Fix-now — narrow D.1, D4’s as-built description and attempt 5’s record to exact-pair withholding; preserve the existing code unless a broader rule is separately intended.

- **PR-LOW-050** (LOW, test reliability, `desktop/tests/test_ui_suggestions.py:589`): The Save test can fail on correct code because result delivery does not guarantee thread disposal. Evidence: `_landed()` waits for `"not screen.suggestion_in_flight and screen._suggest_outcome is not None"` (line 259), followed here by `"assert not screen.is_busy"`. `_JobSlot.release()` appends the reporting job to `_orphaned_jobs`; only the separately delivered `finished` handler removes it. The event pump can return between those signals. Recommendation: Fix-now — retain the landing assertion, then explicitly wait for disposal before asserting that the screen is no longer busy.

- **PR-LOW-051** (LOW, test coverage, `desktop/tests/test_ui_suggestions.py:898`): The yield-wait tests do not establish that the worker entered the wait before closing, so they can exercise abort-before-yield instead of the claimed abort-during-yield path. Evidence: `"stage.release.set()"` followed by `"assert not _process_until(qapp, lambda: stage.windows >= 2, timeout=0.3)"`; the screen-level sibling uses the same pattern at lines 615–616, and the prose-yield test uses a 0.4-second absence check at line 401. A delayed worker satisfies these checks without reaching the yield callback. Recommendation: Fix-now — add an event handshake establishing yield-wait entry before triggering close or releasing prose; keep timeouts only as failure bounds.

### Phase R — The clinical register

Today `i`, `i've`, `i'm`, `me`, `my`, `we` are NOT connectives, so a patient line's prose must keep them: "I've had this pain in my lower back" can become "Pt reports pain in lower back" only if the first person is glue under the frame. Options: (a) **the swap (Recommended)** — under `speaker_frame` the first-person tokens (i, i've, i'm, me, my, mine, we, we've, our; the exact set pinned) are droppable in rule (a), the frame tokens admitted in rule (b): the frame REPLACES the first person and nothing else — the modal contractions `i'd`, `i'll`, `we'd`, `we'll` are NOT in the set (their `would` / `will` is a polarity-bearing fact) and stay verbatim under either option (codex PR-MED-011); (b) frame tokens only — "Pt reports: I've had this pain in my lower back", the first person kept. ### Phase C — Model-suggested sections for left-out lines
- [x] ✅ **C.0 Fix the window slice and the context lines** `[decision]`. Options: (a) `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS` from 0.3's rates so one window with context runs about 30–45 s (the longest wait any exit path or the close guard can see), context lines on; (b) a larger slice (fewer system-prompt re-evaluations, longer waits), named; (c) context lines off (asked lines only) if 0.3's rates put a usable slice with context past the 240 s cap for a 25-minute consultation. Decide after: 0.3. Blocks: C.1. Done when: the constant and the context choice are named with their derivation (the window's expected seconds and count for a 25-minute consultation) as a dated note on D9.
  - **Executor derivation (stage-2 leg x1, 2026-11-09T04:44:25+11:00) — a DRAFT for the practitioner's decision (the MUST-PAUSE in the handoff note); nothing decided, D9 not yet amended.** Formula per window: seconds = prompt tokens / 57.6 + answer tokens / 11.4 (Task 0.3's means; the system prompt is re-evaluated on every window because `_clear_inference_state` drops the cache per call). Inputs, each an ESTIMATE and named as one: (1) the fixed part of every prompt ≈ 560 tokens — `SUGGEST_INSTRUCTION` is not written yet (C.1), so it is estimated from D7 / D9's specification at ≈ 500 (the task ≈ 60; the 17 keys of `note.CANONICAL_SECTIONS` with a one-line meaning and owner hint ≈ 300; the data-never-instructions sentence ≈ 30; the answer form ≈ 40), plus ≈ 20 for the chat template and ≈ 40 for `LINES BEGIN` / `LINES END` and the "Answer one line per `?` number" line; every 100 tokens more adds 1.7 s to every window; (2) segments: a transcript segment is one VAD speech span (`transcription.assemble_transcript`; split on `speech.MIN_SILENCE_SECONDS = 0.35`), and the one recorded rate is the Phase 2 benchmark — 19 segments in 81.9 s of the clinical script, 10 in 53.2 s of the non-clinical text (`plan-phase2-recording-transcription.md:875`; SAPI speech, not a consultation) — so 14 segments a minute, 350 for a 25-minute consultation, ≈ 10 words (≈ 13 tokens) a segment (189 words / 19); real consultations are unmeasured (C5: no real transcript is read); (3) line sizes: a context line `<n> <speaker> [placed: <key>] <text>` ≈ 28 tokens, an asked line `<n>? <speaker> <text>` ≈ 20 (Qwen splits digits; a key is ≈ 5 tokens); (4) left-out (asked) lines ≈ 30 % of the pre-boundary segments, ≈ 105 of 350 — an assumption, unmeasured on real consultations; (5) answer ≈ 8 tokens per asked line + 2 (D7's `max_tokens = 8 × asked + 16`); (6) every pre-boundary segment is in some window when context is on (a slice with no asked line is skipped — C.1's planner, rare at 30 %); the cap is checked BEFORE each window (D9), so a job can end up to one window past 240 s. Results (seconds of model time; the 25-minute line is the plan's question, the 10-minute line for scale):
    - **(a) context on, `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 40`:** a window ≈ (560 + 25 × 40) / 57.6 + (8 × 12 + 2) / 11.4 = 27.1 + 8.6 ≈ **36 s** (inside the 30–45 s target). 25 minutes: 9 windows needed (8 × 40 + 30), ≈ 315 s — PAST the 240 s cap; windows start at 0, 36, … 214 s, so 7 run and the job ends ≈ 250 s; 280 of 350 segments assessed, the last 70 (≈ 21 of the ≈ 105 left-out lines, the consultation's last ≈ 5 minutes) reported not assessed. Consultations up to ≈ 20 minutes (280 segments) are assessed whole. 10 minutes: 4 windows, ≈ 130 s, all assessed.
    - **(b) a larger slice, context on, `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 100`:** a window ≈ (560 + 2,500) / 57.6 + (8 × 30 + 2) / 11.4 = 53.1 + 21.2 ≈ **74 s** (past the 30–45 s target; the prompt 3,060 + `max_tokens` 256 + framing 64 = 3,380 of the 4,096-token context — the context allows about 125 segments at most, so 100 leaves room for longer lines before the D7 halving). 25 minutes: 4 windows (3 × 100 + 50), starting at 0, 74, 149, 223 s — all run, the job ends ≈ 265 s, every segment assessed; up to ≈ 28 minutes (400 segments) assessed whole. 10 minutes: 2 windows, ≈ 110 s.
    - **(c) context off (asked lines only), `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 25` asked lines:** a window ≈ (560 + 20 × 25) / 57.6 + (8 × 25 + 2) / 11.4 = 18.4 + 17.7 ≈ **36 s** (the answer dominates: every line in the window is asked). 25 minutes: 105 asked lines, 5 windows (4 × 25 + 5), ≈ 160 s, all assessed, well under the cap (the cap would bind only past ≈ 175 asked lines). 10 minutes: 2 windows, ≈ 64 s. The cost is routing quality: a short answer ("yeah, about three weeks") is sent without the question it answers unless that question was also left out.
    - **Common to all three:** (i) the result lands ONCE, when the job ends (C.1's one `SuggestionStageResult`; D8's landing), so no mark shows before then — ≈ 4 min 10 s / 4 min 25 s / 2 min 40 s for a 25-minute consultation under (a) / (b) / (c), plus ≈ 10 s for the model's load under Clean clinical (the default style: no prose job has loaded it; whether the load is inside the job's clock is C.1's, the clock starting at the first window), or after the prose job under a prose style (D8 arms the job on its release); (ii) the longest wait any path sees is ONE window — (a) ≈ 36 s, (b) ≈ 74 s, (c) ≈ 36 s — for the window-close guard, a Recovery-tab open-review of another recording, and Save after an Add (the new prose job waits behind the in-flight window, D8 residue (1)); the same figure is how long transcript text stays in memory after Complete or Discard (D8 residue (3)); Complete and Discard themselves never wait (abort and orphan, the key destroyed first — `main_window.py:2066-2078`); about 10 s more if the close lands during the model's first load; (iii) the laptop's speed drifted about 35 % between sessions in Phase R (Last Session): at 35 % slower, (a)'s window is ≈ 48 s and the cap assesses ≈ 14 of 25 minutes; (b)'s ≈ 100 s and ≈ 21 of 25 minutes; (c)'s ≈ 49 s and still all of them (≈ 215 s); (iv) Task 0.3 measured prompt evaluation at about 2,500 tokens — a shorter prompt evaluates somewhat faster per token, so the small windows' figures lean slow.
    - **Executor recommendation: (a) — `SUGGESTION_WINDOW_SEGMENTS = 40`, context lines on.** It meets D9's 30–45 s target; what the cap cuts on a long consultation is its END (windows run in transcript order) — mostly the clinician's own treatment, advice and plan lines, class W, which already carry a left-out-line warning — while the early history, where the silent class U lives, is always assessed; and the context is what lets a short answer be routed at all (D9's Why). Stated plainly: by this estimate a full 25-minute consultation with context needs ≈ 315 s, so D9's trigger for (c) ("if context doubles the time past the cap") is met on paper — (c) is a fair choice if the practitioner would rather every line be looked at than the short answers be routed well. C.6's seen sets are short scripts that fit in one window under every option, so run 9 measures (a) against (c) only through the context choice.

The fix: `NoteScreen(..., suggestion_model_present: Callable[[], bool] | None = None)` — the window passes its `_language_model_available` seam; `_begin_suggestions` asks it ON THE GUI THREAD after the nothing-to-suggest check (the stage's own order) and, when absent, names `suggestion_model_absent_line()` and starts NO thread. So every existing window test (all built through `_main_window`, whose seam says absent, or the default seam over the conftest's empty models root) runs exactly as before C.4. Presence stays a stat asked per review, never kept (round 20 MED-002); the stage still asks it again on its worker (unchanged). No existing `is_busy` / close test needed changing.   - **Diagnosis of the new R3 counts and the wrongly-present facts — (ii) NOT A DEFECT, a measured cost of `confirm-all` (2026-11-09T11:15:01+11:00, leg x8; ids, segment roles and counts only, from the composer's synthetic reports and the code; no `validation/heldout-*` file read).** The NEW R3s are h3-13 (`heldout-3`; h3-17 was R3 under `off` already) and h5-12 (`heldout-5`; h5-10 likewise). In each, the next patient's absent fact (h3-13/f4, h5-12/f5) moves correctly_absent → wrongly_present and is attributed to a suggested line (D10's inclusive rule, `validation.py:2292-2303`); h5-12's ONE addition is the other speaker's line (W 0, U 1), so that line carried it; h3-13 added 7 W and 3 U and the report, counts only by design, does not name which (R3's own term places the carrying statement in the reference tail, `fact.line >= reference.tail_start`, `validation.py:2282`; tails 10 and 16, `:680`, `:703`). Why it is not a defect: R3 holds only when the run's finalised note has NO next-patient boundary (`boundary is None`, `:2283`, read from the note's Check 6 warning by `next_patient_segment`, `:2060`) and the script's own words have none either (`reference.boundary is None`, `:2284`; the reports' reference-boundary line: h3-13 none / 10, h5-12 none / 16); neither note carries `possible_next_patient` (the checker tallies). With no boundary, `eligible_utterances` has nothing to refuse (`ui/models.py:3010-3013` refuses a suggestion only when `boundary is not None and index >= boundary`) — in the harness (`validation.py:3237`, `note_config.next_patient_boundary(document, inputs.config)`) and in the Note tab (`ui/note.py:1466-1468`, `next_patient_segment(note.note_warnings)`) alike, because Check 6's warning is built from that same accessor (`note_check.py:2079-2088`) on the same config (the harness finalises with `inputs.config`); the suggestion input is cut at the same boundary (`build_note_request`, `validation.py:3219-3221`). No encounter WITH a Check 6 boundary has a fact wrongly present via suggestion (e.g. h3-01, h3-02, h3-12: 0). So the suggestion reaches exactly the residue R3 names — an unbroken next patient, bounded by a ceiling — where under `off` the provider happened to leave the tail out. h3-18 (wrongly present via suggestion, NOT R3): the script's words carry a boundary (8, tail 9 — one of the report's listed mismatches) but the run's transcript does not (no `possible_next_patient`): the recogniser broke the boundary phrase, and R3 excuses only a next patient even the script's words cannot find (`:2284`), so it counts as material_wrong — h3-18 PASS → FAIL; the same mechanism, not a defect; its carrying line (of 6 W, 3 U) is not named in the report. Also measured, outside the six columns (D10 counts `wrongly_present` only): two material facts moved omitted → wrong — a misheard line left out under `off` entered through a suggested line (the additions are the only difference between the policies: prose yields counts only and the fact walk reads the finalised note, `validation.py:3371-3387`): h2-15/f9 (not R1, so h2-15 PASS → FAIL material_wrong; h2-15's four additions are the clinician's) and h3-13/f2 (a marked R1 — under `heldout-3`'s ceiling, R1 2 over 1; its unsupported line excused by the R1 exemption). No pin added and no `--only` rerun owed (nothing was fixed). These are the inputs predictions v6 (F.1) carry, and P.6's register rows ("a row per `Wrongly present via suggestion`"); D11 already rejects a predicate change for suggestions, so nothing here reopens a locked decision — the practitioner's reading is the non-blocking note in the handoff.

(2) The seven ratified terms with a rarer second meaning — `ANS`, `PNS`, `Cx`, `C/O`, `RC`, `ROM`, `SLR` — for the CHECK 5 block (round 4 LOW-003, codex PR-LOW-013). (3) The Shorthand withholding rule (`narrative-v7`): `prose_style.shorthand_line` does not offer a `duration` entry's `<phrase> = <term>` pair for a section when any occurrence of that exact phrase is directly followed by a point-in-time word (the pair only — another phrase of the same entry elsewhere in the section is still offered; corrected 2026-11-09T03:21:04+11:00 per codex round 8 PR-LOW-025 / -037) (`POINT_IN_TIME_WORDS` = ago, earlier, later, before, after, prior), because the model folded that word into the shorthand ("six weeks ago" → "6/52", R.6's `syn-05`) and Check 5 rightly refused the lost fact; it narrows what the prompt suggests, never what Check 5 admits — for the CHECK 5 / prose-stage text and flow 17 beside the Shorthand line. NEW (round 7): (4) the frame is per SECTION (`note.section_other_voice`: any transcript line not the confirmed clinician's), so in a MIXED section the clinician's own first-person words are droppable and `Pt` addable too — a clinician's "I tested the knee" can pass as "Pt tested the knee"; the token-not-attachment residue, now reaching who did what. (5) "Other voice" is anyone but the confirmed clinician, while the frame's words are "Pt" / "patient": a carer's, parent's or interpreter's first-person line (already merged into the patient's label by the two-speaker step — AGENTS.md Known Issues) can be framed as the patient's. (6) The duration fold makes a spelled week count the same token as its numeral, so a spelled duration now joins the number-swap form of the token-not-attachment residue ("3 sets for two weeks" → "2 sets for 3/52" passes, where under v2 the spelled "two" had to stay). (4)–(6) are consequences of the locked D2 and R.0 (a), named so the practitioner reads them: no code change, the clinician's reading stays the control.

*To resume in a new session: open a fresh Agent (Ctrl+I), run /start-session, then run /load-plan*
*Sections marked (Only if relevant) can be omitted when genuinely not applicable, but never omit decisions, constraints, verification steps, or integration knowledge that a fresh session would otherwise have to rediscover.*
*For lightweight plans, plans with fewer than 5 tasks, or plans created without a preceding `/explore` discussion, sections with no real content should be marked `N/A — simple feature` rather than padded with placeholder structure.*
*State-once convention (new plans): state each cross-section contract — a critical constraint, design decision, invariant, or other normative rule — once in its canonical section, and have every other section point to it rather than restate it. Scope guard: this governs cross-section normative duplication only — the Task Specification Standard (in `/create-plan` and `/review-plan`) and task-local detail are untouched, and a plan recording `fast/medium involved` in its Executor tier may keep extra restatement where it helps the fast executor. It applies to new plans as they are written; never retroactively rewrite an existing plan to conform.*
